```text
workers 001-004:

worker 005:
  primary future test/general worker

```

This **supersedes** the old plan instruction:

```text
move worker-001 to MIAM00100
```

Do not attempt that migration.

## 1.2 Worker placement policy

Preferred future placement:

```text
1. MIAM00100 for normal/general workers
2. other eligible non-critical Proxmox nodes as capacity requires
3. inference nodes only intentionally, max 1 AI worker per inference node
```

Never use for general agent workers:

```text
MIAM00133
MIAM00147
```

Inference-node rule:

```text
MIAM00111 → max 1 AI agent
MIAM00112 → max 1 AI agent
MIAM00143 → max 1 AI agent
MIAM00144 → max 1 AI agent
```

The current 001-004 topology intentionally consumes those four slots.

## 1.3 Durable naming

Human-required worker naming convention:

```text
acms-hermes-worker-uid-001
acms-hermes-worker-uid-002
acms-hermes-worker-uid-003
acms-hermes-worker-uid-004
acms-hermes-worker-uid-005
```

Reason:

```text
acms      = orchestration domain
hermes    = execution harness
worker    = role
uid-###   = durable unique worker identifier
```

Future non-Hermes harnesses can use a different harness segment.

Example:

```text
acms-codex-worker-uid-006
```

The UID is the durable identity.

VM ID, node, IP, hostname and runtime instance are not durable agent identity.

# 2. Safe Naming Migration — Do Not Destroy Existing Agent History

Current ACMS rows already exist as:

```text
acms-worker-001
acms-worker-002
acms-worker-003
acms-worker-004
acms-worker-005
```

Existing UUIDs must be preserved.

Do **not** delete/re-register agents merely to get prettier names.

The prior session proved there is no current rename endpoint.

Implement a safe compatibility model.

## 2.1 Preferred data model

Add/confirm fields such as:

```text
agent_id / UUID             immutable
worker_uid                  immutable, e.g. 001
harness                     hermes
display_name                acms-hermes-worker-uid-001
legacy_name                 acms-worker-001
runtime_instance_id         mutable
runtime_hostname            mutable
node                         mutable
vmid                         mutable
ip                           mutable
```

The existing UUID is the real database identity.

Human/UI/API canonical display should become:

```text
acms-hermes-worker-uid-###
```

Legacy names may remain aliases for compatibility.

## 2.2 If schema change is too large for this session

Minimum safe fallback:

- keep existing DB rows and UUIDs;
- add an alias/display-name capability;
- show new names in UI;
- use new name for Hermes profile;
- keep old name only as legacy external identifier;
- document alias mapping.

Do **not** block worker completion on a cosmetic destructive rename.

# 6. GLM 5.3 / Hermes Steering Failure Notes

This section is important.

The previous session demonstrates a model-operating failure mode.

## 6.1 What happened

The original plan said:

```text
migrate worker-001 to MIAM00100
```

Human gave mid-session steering that changed the architecture to:

```text
leave worker-001 on MIAM00111
create 002/003/004 on other inference hosts
create 005 on MIAM00100
use acms-hermes-worker-uid-### naming
```

The model partially acted on the steer.

Later it lost provenance for those steering instructions and wrote that the directives were "unverified."

Then a later response itself introduced unrelated/nonexistent context such as:

```text
HH-4
PVD agent
```

The handoff explicitly identifies that final "pause" message as a confusion artifact.

## 6.2 Root problem

Likely failure pattern:

```text
long execution
+ original plan
+ several runtime discoveries
+ mid-session architecture changes
+ conversational steer
+ no durable steer ledger
= model loses which instruction supersedes which
```

This is not a reason to distrust verified tool output.

It is a reason to change how we steer long GLM/Hermes sessions.

---

# 7. New Steering Protocol for GLM 5.3 / Hermes

Use this protocol in future long execution sessions.

## 7.1 Never apply a major steer silently

When receiving a steer, classify it as one of:

```text
A. CLARIFICATION
B. PRIORITY CHANGE
C. PLAN AMENDMENT
D. STOP / ROLLBACK
E. FYI ONLY
```

A worker-placement change is:

```text
PLAN AMENDMENT
```

## 7.2 Write every plan amendment durably

Maintain:

```text
STEER-LOG.md
```

Each steer entry:

```text
STEER-ID:
Timestamp:
User text:
Classification:
Affected plan sections:
Old instruction:
New instruction:
Supersedes:
Does NOT change:
Work already executed:
Rollback needed:
Next safe checkpoint:
```

## 7.3 Update PLAN-OF-RECORD.md

After a major steer:

1. finish current atomic transaction if safer than stopping halfway;
2. stop at a checkpoint;
3. update the plan-of-record;
4. show the delta;
5. then continue.

Do not keep the old plan and the new steer as equally authoritative.

## 7.4 Echo the interpretation before executing a major architecture steer

Use concise acknowledgement:

```text
STEER RECEIVED

Old:
worker-001 → MIAM00100

New:
keep worker-001 → MIAM00111
create worker-005 → MIAM00100

I will not migrate worker-001.
I will provision worker-005 instead.
```

Then execute.

## 7.5 Never invent missing steering context

If transcript/context does not contain a directive:

```text
UNKNOWN
```

Do not manufacture an explanation.

Ask or consult `STEER-LOG.md`.

## 7.6 Verified state beats conversational recollection

Use authority order:

```text
1. live system/tool output
2. current PLAN-OF-RECORD.md
3. STEER-LOG.md
4. latest handoff
5. conversational recollection
```

## 7.7 New session starts from files, not memory

Every fresh Hermes session must read:

```text
PLAN-OF-RECORD.md
STEER-LOG.md
latest HANDOFF.md
ROLLBACK.md
```

before mutation.

## 7.8 One checkpoint after each major phase

At checkpoint write:

```text
DONE
PARTIAL
NOT STARTED
BLOCKED
PLAN AMENDMENTS
CURRENT LIVE STATE
NEXT 3 ACTIONS
```

(1) Self-\n  hosted runners REUSE RUNNER_TEMP — stale `llm-manager-*.tar.gz` survive jobs\n  and `find | head -1` picks them: always rm stale tarballs + select by EXACT\n  name (short-sha form — see the release-transaction section). (2) Do NOT `rm\n  artifact.zip` in the unpack step — the download step writes it fresh; rm-ing\n  it after download kills unzip (\"cannot find or open artifact.zip\"). Wrong node → 500 \"qemu-server/N.conf\n  does not exist\".\n- Two-step: `POST /nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/agent/exec` (returns pid) → sleep\n  → `GET .../agent/exec-status?pid=<pid>` (out-data/err-data/exitcode).\n- Stage scripts as base64 (≤9KB chunks) written to a temp file, then decoded\n  and run. VM114 qga has a wedge risk on long execs — keep each exec\n  minimal, use nohup + log-poll for long operations.\n- **VM114 has root SSH too** (`ssh vm114` alias via `~/.ssh/config.d/`, dedicated\n  key) — simpler than qga for file staging (`scp` tarballs/probes to /tmp) and\n  log polling. Deploy-flow exceptions where qga/node-SSH is still required:\n  see `vm114-qga-ssh-recovery` skill.\n\n## ARM reconciler semantics (services/reconciliation.py)\n\n- desired RUNNING: observe → start owned VM once (ownership-verified, bounded\n  retries w/ capped backoff, `recovery_count`) → RUNNING; PVE truth corrects\n  stale DB actuals.\n- desired STOPPED: graceful shutdown once → **bounded convergence wait**\n  (`ARM_RECONCILE_SHUTDOWN_WAIT_S`, default 30s/3s polls). Backoff 10/20/40/60s capped\n  (attempts vary). Every attempt audits `failure_class` /\n  `recovery_method` / `attempt` in the RuntimeEvent detail (durable audit log\n  IS the recovery-method history — no second mutable table). Exhaustion\n  audits `strategy_next: human_attention`. Ladder: recheck/observe → VM start\n  ×5 → Human Attention. {"success": true, "name": "proxmox-cluster-infrastructure", "description": "Build, configure, and verify Proxmox VE / Proxmox Datacenter Manager infrastructure: PVE API login, LXC provisioning, PDM setup, LLDAP access, remote cluster registration, no-subscription repo setup, and subscription-nag mitigation.", "tags": [], "related_skills": [], "content": "---\nname: proxmox-cluster-infrastructure\ndescription: \"Build, configure, and verify Proxmox VE / Proxmox Datacenter Manager infrastructure: PVE API login, LXC provisioning, PDM setup, LLDAP access, remote cluster registration, no-subscription repo setup, and subscription-nag mitigation.\"\n---\n\n# Proxmox Cluster Infrastructure\n\nUse this skill when asked to administer a Proxmox VE cluster, deploy Proxmox Datacenter Manager (PDM), add PVE nodes/remotes to PDM, configure LDAP/LLDAP access, or fix PDM post-install UI/repository issues.\n\n## Safety and boundaries\n\n- Treat Proxmox/PDM credentials, API tickets, CSRF tokens, generated API tokens, LDAP bind passwords, and root passwords as sensitive. Do **not** print them in final responses or save them in skills/memory.\n- Prefer API verification over GUI assumptions. If the GUI behavior is reported by the user, verify via API wherever possible, then state when a browser hard refresh/cache clear is still required.\n- When using community scripts, inspect the current raw script first and apply only the needed sections when running interactively is impractical.\n- For destructive operations (destroying CTs/VMs, replacing remotes, modifying ACLs), verify the target node/VMID/remote ID first and avoid affecting unrelated workloads.\n- **Node power boundaries (Jordan, 2026-09-11, explicit directive):** NEVER power off or restart `miam-00133` or `miam-00135` — they run critical services and the Hermes bot VMs (906 etc.). VM/guest restarts are fine; node restarts are not. `miam-00147` (and its ThunderBay via PDU 153 outlets 7/8) may be power-cycled for qualification work. Confirm live outlet labels in the PDU portal before every cycle.\n\n## Proxmox VE API login pattern\n\n1. Discover the authentication realm before assuming username format:\n   - `GET /api2/json/access/domains`\n   - Common working form for LLDAP-backed users is `username@LLDAP-Domain`.\n2. Authenticate:\n   - `POST /api2/json/access/ticket` with `username` and `password`.\n   - Store `PVEAuthCookie` in the session and send `CSRFPreventionToken` for POST/PUT/DELETE.\n3. Verify access with read-only endpoints:\n   - `GET /api2/json/version`\n   - `GET /api2/json/nodes`\n   - `GET /api2/json/access/permissions`\n4. Redact ticket and CSRF values in logs/summaries.\n\n### PVE API via LLDAP bot account — PRIMARY control path (proven 2026-09-10, user-directed)\n\nJordan set this as the primary access path (over pexpect/SSH): drive the PVE API with\nthe LLDAP bot account. Ready-made helper: `scripts/pve_api.py` (creds from a 600 file,\ntwo lines: username, password; default `~/.pve_ldap_bot`; cluster endpoint override via\n`PVE_HOST`).\n\n- Login: POST `/api2/json/access/ticket` with `username=<bot>@LLDAP-Domain` -> response\n  carries `ticket` + `CSRFPreventionToken`. Send cookie `PVEAuthCookie` on every call;\n  send `CSRFPreventionToken` header on non-GETs. `/access/domains` lists the realm\n  (`LLDAP-Domain`).\n- **Node short-names are MIXED across the cluster** — `miam00111/miam00112/miam00143/\n  miam00144` (no hyphen) vs `miam-00135` etc. (hyphen). Always enumerate\n  `GET /api2/json/nodes` and use the exact returned name; guessing yields\n  `500 hostname lookup failed`.\n- **Guest exec (the replacement for `qm guest exec`):** POST\n  `/nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/agent/exec` with body\n  `{\"command\":[\"bash\",\"-lc\",\"echo <b64> | base64 -d | bash\"]}`, then poll\n  GET `/nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/agent/exec-status?pid=<pid>` until `exited`; read\n  `exitcode` / `out-data` / `err-data`. TWO traps: (1) urlencode the body with\n  `doseq=True` — a plain urlencode silently DROPS list values, and the exec fails with\n  HTTP 596 / no pid, which looks like a broken guest agent but is a client bug;\n  (2) dispatch returns 500 when the VM is stopped — check vm status first.\n- Guest agent channel sanity probe: GET `/agent/get-osinfo` (cheap, works even when\n  exec routing misbehaves). VM stopped -> 500 with no data.\n- **PVE API parameter formats (2026-09-13, v0.11 session):** three distinct traps in one\n  endpoint family. (1) Array params are REPEATED plain keys, NOT `name[]=` — body\n  `command=bash&command=-lc&command=<script>`; `command[]=...` and JSON-string bodies both\n  400 with `Parameter verification failed`. (2) `agent/exec` does NOT accept a `timeout`\n  param (schema rejects it: `property is not defined in schema`) — dispatch returns a pid\n  and you enforce your own deadline while polling. (3) `/agent/exec` returns\n  `{\"data\":{\"pid\": N}}` (a DICT) — extracting `data['pid']` blindly throws; handle both\n  dict and bare-int shapes. (4) GET endpoints take params in the QUERYSTRING — passing\n  them as a POST-style body yields silent empty responses / 400s.\n- **LXC containers have NO `/lxc/<id>/exec` API endpoint on PVE 9.2** (POST returns\n  `501 Method 'POST /nodes/.../lxc/.../exec' not implemented`) — the qemu guest-agent exec\n  path is VM-only. For CT internals use SSH/node-shell (`pct exec`), or reach the CT's\n  services over the network from a VM that has the tooling (e.g. install\n  `postgresql-client` on the app VM and dump remotely instead of exec'ing into the DB CT).\n- **`pct exec <id> -- bash -c \"<script>\"` breaks on complex scripts** (2026-09-30 inventory\n  sweep): the script text crosses TWO shell layers (node SSH + pct exec), and `$`/backtick/quote\n  escaping that survives both is fragile — batched scans silently returned 59-byte empty outputs\n  with no error. Working pattern: base64 the script → write it to node `/tmp` → run it INSIDE\n  the CT from a file via stdin: `echo <b64> | base64 -d > /tmp/s_<id>.sh && pct exec <id> --\n  bash < /tmp/s_<id>.sh > /tmp/out_<id>.txt 2>&1; cat /tmp/out_<id>.txt`. Also: `pct exec` does\n  NOT reliably forward a piped stdin from the SSH command line — always go through a node-side file.\n- **Long guest commands through qga exec: run detached + result file.** qga exec (CLI or API)\n  blocks ~2 min and times out on sleeps. For \"wait N seconds then report\" probes, dispatch\n  `nohup bash -c '... > /tmp/result.txt 2>&1 &'` (returns \"started\" immediately), then poll with\n  a second exec that cats the result file.\n- **Root@pam ticket via `/access/ticket` works from a Python urllib client** when the\n  bash+curl path is unavailable (no jq / curl quirks) — same urlencoded form\n  (`username=root@pam`), same cookie/CSRF handling, self-signed TLS needs an\n  `ssl.CERT_NONE` context. Reusable pattern: stage creds in a 0600 two-line file, refresh\n  the ticket on 401 once, then proceed.\n- **`vmstatus` reads `/nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/status/current`; `vmconfig` reads\n  `/nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/config`. Both safe read-only.**\n- If cluster-forwarded API calls fail oddly (e.g. HTTP 596), retry against the node's\n  own endpoint `https://10.0.20.<node-digits>:8006` with the same bot ticket.\n\n### PDM API login differs from PVE (pitfall)\nProxmox Datacenter Manager is a SEPARATE service (not plain PVE); its auth is NOT the\nsame as a PVE cluster node login:\n- Endpoint is `https://<pdm-host>:8443/api2/json/access/ticket` (port 8443, not 8006).\n- Username form is `user@LLDAP-Domain` (same realm discovery trick).\n- The success payload keys are **`ticket-info` and `CSRFPreventionToken`** — there is NO\n  `ticket` key. Do not read `data['ticket']`. The `data` value may arrive as a\n  JSON-encoded STRING on some versions — parse defensively (`if isinstance(d, str):\n  d = json.loads(d)`), or a dict access throws `'str' object has no attribute 'get'`.\n- The auth cookie is **`__Host-PDMAuthCookie`** (a `__Host-` prefixed cookie), NOT\n  `PVEAuthCookie`. Persist it in a cookie jar (`curl -c/-b`) rather than hand-setting.\n- Passing the cookie to `GET /api2/json/remotes/remote` works (node list is enumerated in\n  the remote's `nodes` array as `ip,fingerprint=...`). Aggregate read endpoints like\n  `GET /api2/json/resources/list` are available; but fine-grained config (e.g. a VM's\n  `qm config`) is NOT proxied through PDM — reach the node's PVE host directly\n  (`/nodes/<node>/<resource>/<vmid>/config`) or SSH to the host for `qm config`.\n  - `resources/list` shape: `{\"data\":[{\"remote\":\"<remote-id>\",\"resources\":[...]}]}` — one\n    element per remote; entries are `pve-node` / `pve-storage` / `pve-qemu` / `pve-lxc`\n    with `vmid,node,status,name,tags`. Ideal for cluster-wide VMID/IP/name audits.\n  - Login also works as `jordatech@LLDAP-Domain`; use a curl `-c`/`-b` cookie jar (the\n    `__Host-PDMAuthCookie`) instead of hand-built Authorization headers.\n- `GET /api2/json/nodes` returns `permission check failed` for a limited LDAP account;\n  rely on `remotes/remote` + `resources/list` for node/guest enumeration instead.\n- If your PVE host layer needs full stack detail, SSH root to the host and run `qm`\n  directly — simpler and permission-complete than fighting PDM's API surface.\n\n### PVE API as PRIMARY access path (user directive 2026-09-11: \"Switch primary to the PVE API/web path\")\n\nUse the PVE REST API through an LLDAP bot account as the FIRST choice for cluster\noperations; password-SSH runners (noderun.py) drop to fallback for host-only tasks\nthe API can't reach.\n\n- **Bot creds**: staged as a 0600 file (`~/.pve_ldap_bot`: line 1 = username, line 2 =\n  password; realm suffix appended in code). Never echo, never commit.\n- **Auth**: `POST /api2/json/access/ticket` with urlencoded\n  `username=<user>@LLDAP-Domain&password=...` → then send cookie `PVEAuthCookie` on every\n  call + header `CSRFPreventionToken` for non-GET. TLS is self-signed → ssl context with\n  `check_hostname=False, verify_mode=CERT_NONE`.\n- **Ready-made helper**: `scripts/pve_api.py` (login / realms / nodes / resources /\n  permissions / vmconfig <node> <vmid> / vmstatus <node> <vmid> / raw <METHOD> <path> [body];\n  also importable: `from pve_api import api, load_creds`).\n- **Node short-names are INCONSISTENT** — ring nodes are `miam00111/miam00112/miam00143/\n  miam00144` (no hyphen) while `miam-00135`, `miam-00100`, `miam-00149` carry hyphens.\n  NEVER guess a node name in an API path; enumerate `GET /api2/json/nodes` first.\n- **Guest exec via API** (replaces qm guest exec + most SSH-to-VM work):\n  `POST /nodes/<node>/qemu/<vmid>/agent/exec` with body\n  `{\"command\": [\"bash\", \"-lc\", \"echo <b64-of-script> | base64 -d | bash\"]}`, then poll\n  `GET .../agent/exec-status?pid=<pid>` until `exited` (fields: exitcode, out-data,\n  err-data). **PITFALL: urlencode the body with `doseq=True`** — the `command` value is a\n  LIST, and plain urlencode silently DROPS list values, so the POST returns 200-ish while\n  the exec never runs (dispatch returns no pid / 596-looking failures).\n- **VM lifecycle** via `/nodes/<n>/qemu/<id>/status/start` (and stop/reboot) is clean and\n  audited by the bot identity; track completion via `/nodes/<n>/tasks/<upid>/status`.\n- **PDU power portal** (Jordan, 2026-09-11): web-managed PDU portal at\n  `https://10.0.20.154/` (nginx front; gunicorn binds 127.0.0.1:5000 — :5000 from outside\n  is refused; `http://` 301s to https). Creds staged in `~/.pdu_portal` (600).\n  **Login quirks:** the LLDAP username/password form REJECTED the staged root creds\n  (2026-09-11); the **emergency local administrator** form works — POST `/login` with\n  `mode=emergency`, `e_user`, `e_pass`. The post-login redirect Location is RELATIVE\n  (`/`) — resolve against the base URL or requests/urllib throws MissingSchema. The\n  first dashboard GET can exceed 10s; use a 30s timeout. Outlet rows carry `data-ip`,\n  `data-outlet`, and a label div (153/7 = MIAM-00147 NUC, 153/8 = MIAM-00148 ThunderBay)\n  — verify live labels before cycling. Control-plane only — power telemetry polling\n  stays on Emporia/GPU NVML per the standing rule (never poll PDU network endpoints\n  .151-.153 for telemetry).\n\n### PVE host/guest shell without sshpass (pexpect) + cloud-init & serial recipes\n\nWhen you must drive Proxmox hosts/VMs from a LAN Hermes box (no web GUI, no existing\ninjected key for the host, possibly no DHCP on the segment, optionally offline hosts),\nthe reusable recipes live in `references/pve-guest-vm-operations.md`, and a ready-made\ndrain-safe password-SSH script runner is `scripts/ssh_run.py` (runs a local .sh on a\nnode, saves output; reads the credential from the profile memory file at runtime and\nnever prints it). Key points:\n- pexpect (user-level `~/ssh-venv`) replaces missing `sshpass`; key-based variant for VMs.\n  Ready-made drain-loop runner: `scripts/ssh_run.py` (see scripts dir; handles the\n  empty/truncated `child.before` pitfall and reads the password from profile memory).\n  **Password auth REQUIRES pexpect** — `subprocess.run(..., input=PW)` fails silently\n  (Permission denied) because the password prompt appears AFTER stdin is consumed.\n  If `sshpass` is absent and uninstalled, port the loop to pexpect, never to input-feed.\n- ssh_run.py takes **IP addresses or full resolvable names, not bare node short-names**\n  (`miam00112` → \"Could not resolve hostname\"; use `10.0.20.112`).\n- **PITFALL: credential-extraction regexes coupling scripts to memory formatting.**\n  ssh_run-style scripts that parse the profile MEMORY.md for the password (`r\"root/(\\S+?)\\)\"`\n  etc.) break when memory entries are rewritten (a batch prune changed one entry's\n  phrasing and every helper died with `AssertionError: credential not found`). Make the\n  regex resilient (match `root/<word>` anywhere: `r\"root/(\\w+)\"`), or read the credential\n  from a dedicated file the scripts own — and after any memory rewrite, smoke-test one\n  helper before batch-driving the fleet.\n- **Never treat a `[Pp]assword` match after login as auth rejection** — `qm config`\n  prints a literal `cipassword: ****` line and VM login loops print `Password:`; check for\n  `Permission denied` instead, or you'll report false auth failures.\n- Host SSH rate-limits rapid reconnects → pace calls, batch commands into one base64'd\n  script run once.\n- **PITFALL: pexpect `child.before` empty/truncated on long batched output.** A single\n  `expect(EOF)` + one `child.before` read can return EMPTY (match at buffer start) or\n  lose most output. Drain instead: loop\n  `expect([r'[Pp]assword:', pexpect.EOF, pexpect.TIMEOUT])` until EOF, appending\n  `child.before` on every iteration, then `\"\".join()` the pieces. Keep the password\n  pattern inside the loop so late prompts still match; treat success as the absence of\n  `Permission denied` in the joined text.\n- Large binaries to offline hosts: serve via `python3 -m http.server` on the internet-\n  capable LAN host, `curl` inside the Proxmox host. pexpect-scp with `encoding='utf-8'`\n  corrupts binaries.\n\n### `qm guest exec` / `pct exec` / `pct push` only work on the OWNING node\n\n`/etc/pve` is cluster-readable, but guest-exec commands resolve the guest config against\nthe LOCAL node. Running `qm guest exec 103` from a node that doesn't own VM103 fails with\n`Configuration file 'nodes/<wrong-node>/qemu-server/103.conf' does not exist`. SSH to the\nowning node first (fleet map: VM103→miam-00111, VM401→miam-00112, VM109→miam-00143,\nVM111→miam-00144; CT115→miam-00147). `pct push` additionally requires the CT to be running.\n\n**PITFALL: `qm guest exec <vmid> -- bash -s` does NOT forward stdin.** A heredoc piped at\n`qm guest exec` feeds the node-side qm process, not the guest — the call returns\n`{\"exitcode\":0}` with EMPTY `out-data` while the guest script never ran. This silently\nno-ops entire batch installs. The reliable pattern is base64-in-command:\n`qm guest exec <vmid> -- bash -c \"echo <base64> | base64 -d | bash\"`.\nSame class of confusion: `scp node:/tmp/x` lands the file on the NODE — to move a file\nnode→CT use `pct push <ctid> /tmp/x /tmp/x` (and `pct pull` to fetch out).\n\n### Inspecting the GUEST vs the NODE (verify your vantage point)\n\nA script run directly through SSH lands on the NODE shell — its output (`pve-root` df,\nQEMU process list, host `lspci`, missing `nvidia-smi`) describes the hypervisor, NOT the\nguest. This burned a full diagnostic round: a \"bare guest\" verdict was drawn from node-shell\noutput while the guest was actually fully built (drivers + venv + model cache intact).\nRule: every guest-internal probe (cloud-init status, nvidia-smi, venv/model dirs, guest FS\nusage, guest internet) MUST be wrapped in `qm guest exec <vmid> -- bash -lc '...'` on the\nowning node. Before mutating anything based on an inspection, state which vantage point\nproduced the evidence.\n\n### Deploying a file INTO a VM guest when your box has no direct key\n\n1. base64 the deploy script locally; run it to the owning node over SSH, decode to /tmp there.\n2. From the node: `scp /tmp/x.sh <guest-user>@<guest-ip>:/tmp/` — the node holds the SSH\n   keypair baked into the guest's cloud-init `authorized_keys` (e.g. the `root@miam-00135`\n   RSA key referenced in cloud-config users).\n3. From the node: `ssh <guest-user>@<guest-ip> \"sudo bash /tmp/deploy.sh\"` — the cloud-init\n   user has NOPASSWD sudo. Direct SSH from your own box only works if YOUR key is in the\n   guest's authorized_keys; otherwise this node-relay path is the reliable route.\n\n### Debian 12 LXCs ship no sudo\n\n`sudo: command not found` inside a fresh Debian 12 CT. As root use\n`runuser -u <user> -- <cmd>` (e.g. `runuser -u postgres -- psql ...`). Locale/UTF-8 perl\nwarnings in these CTs are harmless noise.\n\n### Reconstructing an undocumented service into systemd from bash_history\n\nWhen a guest runs a critical service (e.g. vLLM) with no unit file, recover the final\nbattle-tested invocation from `grep -ihE '<service>' /root/.bash_history /home/*/.bash_history`\n—the LAST occurrence is the current working config. Turn it into\n`/etc/systemd/system/<svc>.service` (Environment lines for exports like HF_HOME, ExecStart\nwith all flags, Restart=on-failure, TimeoutStartSec generous for model load), then\n`daemon-reload && enable --now`, and verify via the service's HTTP endpoint\n(`/v1/models` + a small chat completion for vLLM) before calling it up.\n- Read a headless guest's console via `socat - UNIX-CONNECT:/var/run/qemu-server/<vmid>.serial0`\n  under `pty.fork()` — the only view before the guest agent/network are up. Verified\n  working pattern: `pty.fork()` + `os.execvp('socat', ...)`, write `\\n`, then\n  `select()`-loop reads for ~20s; a booting/idle guest yields the `login:` banner.\n  Wrap it in a script landed on the node via base64-heredoc, then `python3 <script>`\n  — nodes have no pexpect installed (stdlib `pty`/`select` only).\n- **cloud-init on the Ubuntu noble image applies network/creds ONLY on first boot.**\n  Bake `ipconfig0/ciuser/cipassword/sshkeys` BEFORE `qm start` on a freshly created VM.\n  Patching a booted VM then `qm reboot` does NOT re-apply them.\n  ### Re-run WITHOUT recreating: bump the cloud-init instance-id\n  When a first boot half-applied (ssh/packages missing, wrong IP) you do NOT need to\n  destroy/rebuild: edit the user snippet (`/var/lib/vz/snippets/<name>.yaml`), bump\n  `instance-id` in the meta snippet (e.g. `bootstrap-v2` → `v3`), set static IP via\n  `qm set <vmid> --ipconfig0 ip=<ip>/24,gw=<gw>`, then `qm stop <vmid> && qm start <vmid>`\n  (start regenerates the cloud-init ISO; the instance-id change forces cloud-init to\n  re-run per-instance modules). Poll readiness with `timeout 2 bash -c 'echo > /dev/tcp/<ip>/22'`\n  + `qm agent <vmid> ping`, then verify `cloud-init status --long` = done/no errors.\n  ### Static vs DHCP on vmbr0: VERIFY befo

{"success": true, "name": "acms-project-operations", "file": "references/hermes-019-worker-api-surface-2026-10-01.md", "content": "# Hermes 0.19.0 worker api-server surface — verified live 2026-10-01\n\nProbed against acms-worker-001 (VM124 @ 10.0.20.203:8402, hermes-agent 0.19.0\nin `/opt/hermes-venv`, gateway = `hermes-bridge.service` running\n`hermes gateway run --profile acms-worker-001 --replace`). Everything below\nwas verified with real HTTP calls from the ACMS container; the worker needed\nZERO changes for the A2A production path.\n\n## Machine-readable capability list\n`GET /v1/capabilities` → auth, `runtime.mode=server_agent`, feature flags\n(run_events_sse, run_stop, tool_progress_events, session_chat_streaming,\nsession_fork, skills_api, …), and an `endpoints` map with exact paths/methods.\nProbe this FIRST instead of guessing routes.\n\n## Run lifecycle (the A2A ACK)\n- `POST /v1/runs` body `{input:[{role,content}], metadata, title, model?}` →\n  **202 `{run_id, status:\"started\"}`** — the run_id IS the worker ACK\n  (ADR-0013 contract).\n- `GET /v1/runs/{run_id}` → `{status: running|completed|failed|cancelled,\n  output, usage{input_tokens,output_tokens,total_tokens}, last_event,\n  session_id, created_at, updated_at}`. **Run statuses are TTL'd** — old run\n  ids 404 after a while; don't treat a 404 as \"never ran\".\n- `POST /v1/runs/{run_id}/stop` = interrupt/cancel (one endpoint).\n- **Model-field caveat:** the request `model` only takes effect through\n  `platforms.api_server.extra.model_routes` (alias → `{model, provider?,\n  api_key?, base_url?}`) in the worker profile config. An UNMATCHED alias is\n  silently ignored → the agent runs the profile config default. Verified live:\n  `model=qwen3.8-flash-next` without a model_routes entry ran `fast`\n  (= qwen3.6). Worker-side fix = add model_routes entries; ACMS-side =\n  still send the effective model in the request body\n  (`bridge.send_work(model=...)`) so the intent is auditable.\n\n## Run events SSE — `GET /v1/runs/{run_id}/events`\ntext/event-stream, `data: {json}` lines, ~30s `: keepalive` comments, stream\ncloses after the terminal event. Verified payload shapes:\n- `run.completed` `{output, usage{input_tokens,output_tokens,total_tokens}}`\n- `run.failed` `{error (redacted)}`, `run.cancelled`\n- `tool.started` `{tool, preview}`, `tool.completed` `{tool, duration, error}`\n- `message.delta` `{delta}`, `reasoning.available` `{text}`\nSubscribe right after POST (handler waits up to 1s for stream registration).\n`reasoning.available` carries hidden model reasoning — ACMS drops it\n(STEA-004 plan §14: never expose non-user-visible reasoning).\n\n## Session chat stream — `POST /api/sessions/{id}/chat/stream`\nSSE events: run.started, message.started, assistant.delta, tool.progress,\ntool.started/completed/failed, assistant.completed, run.completed, error,\ndone. Response header `X-Hermes-Session-Id` echoes the session id.\n\n## Health / status (heartbeat sources)\n- `GET /health/detailed` → `{status, readiness.checks{state_db, config,\n  model, disk{used_percent,free_bytes}, gateway{state,connected_platforms},\n  background_queues{active_api_runs,process_completions,\n  active_delegations}}, active_agents, gateway_busy, gateway_drainable,\n  platforms.api_server{state}, version, updated_at}`.\n  `active_agents > 0 or gateway_busy` ⇒ worker is executing.\n- `GET /api/sessions` rows carry: id, model, title, started_at/ended_at,\n  message_count, tool_call_count, input_tokens, output_tokens,\n  cache_read_tokens, cache_write_tokens, reasoning_tokens,\n  estimated_cost_usd, actual_cost_usd, api_call_count, last_active, preview —\n  one call yields session identity + token usage + cost for the heartbeat\n  payload (what `telemetry_scheduler._fetch_worker_status` consumes).\n- `GET /api/sessions/{id}/messages` → role, content, reasoning, token_count,\n  timestamp (transcript excerpts for Context Markdown).\n\n## Route truth for model-policy chains (ACMS side)\nServer Manager (VM114 :8300) `GET /api/v1/model-routes` (svc-token auth —\n`svc-server-manager` from service_tokens; split `token:scopes` on the FIRST\ncolon) returns every logical route with `{engine, backend_url, health,\nroutable, is_alias, alias_target, context_limit}`. Healthy locals =\n`routable && !is_alias && health==\"healthy\" && engine != openrouter`.\nThis is what `acms/model_policy.py::fetch_healthy_local_models()` consumes —\nLLM Manager owns route truth; ACMS never maintains its own health copy.", "file_type": ".md"}

## 2026-09-27 (06:15 UTC) — llm-manager-project-framework: CI/CD pipeline hardened live on staging; release transaction reaches ACCEPTED end-to-end

- **4 more PRs merged (#18–#22), all 7 CI checks green:** (18) healthcheck probed PHANTOM `/api/benchmarks` → real `/api/history/benchmarks`; (19) `deploy-release.sh` broke when invoked by absolute path (`./$(dirname $0)`); (20) **PROD DB IP 10.0.20.116 was a hard-coded default in deploy-release.sh + preflight.sh** — a staging migration attempt pointed at prod, stopped only by missing credentials. Resolution order now env > web-unit systemd Environment > 127.0.0.1; (21+22) migrate.py mirrors the app's PG credential contract (`PG_PW=` line in `/etc/llm-manager/secrets/pg_app_creds`), empty env falls through.
- **PROOF (06:07 UTC):** artifact from main tip → preflight PASS → candidate install → validation → migrations applied vs LOCAL staging DB (ledger v001) → atomic activate → ordered restarts (recovery LAST) → healthcheck 13/13 PASS → **Release ACCEPTED (2e2c3360c55d)**. Staging live on main tip.
- **The earlier e5fe98d FAILED transaction (05:21–05:31) was the pipeline working:** strict-old healthcheck failed → auto-rollback fired → rollback healthcheck also failed (old-script bug) → ledger recorded `rollback_degraded` → manual recovery → pipeline hardened. First real-world exercise of plan §14.
- **Lesson (chicken-and-egg):** deploy tooling fixes only reach staging THROUGH the tooling. When transaction scripts themselves change, unpack the new artifact to /tmp and run ITS deploy-release.sh against its own tarball — the artifact self-hosts its tooling.
- **Secret-exposure tally for rotation decision:** 3 independent in-session exposures of LiteLLM master key / llmmanager DSN (VM114 archive capture, subagent terminal, session transcript). None persisted to disk or repo. ROTATE at next window.
- Staging CT120: 4 releases retained, current=2e2c336, /tmp artifacts cleaned. Handoff addendum 2 appended in ~/llm-manager-cicd-20260927/HANDOFF-LLM-MANAGER-CICD-VM102-20260927.md.

os.path.expanduser(\"~/.pve_ldap_bot\")\n\n\ndef load_creds():\n    with open(CREDS) as f:\n        lines = [l.rstrip(\"\\n\") for l in f if l.strip()]\n    return lines[0], lines[1]\n\n\ndef body=None):\n    data = None\n    if isinstance(body, dict):\n        data = urllib.parse.urlencode(body, doseq=True).encode()\n    elif isinstance(body, str):\n        data = body.encode()\n    r = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=data, method=method)\n    if ticket:\n        r.add_header(\"Cookie\", \"PVEAuthCookie=\" + ticket)\n    if csrf and method not in (\"GET\",):\n        r.add_header(\"CSRFPreventionToken\", csrf)\n    try:\n        with urllib.request.urlopen(r, context=CTX, timeout=20) as resp:\n            return resp.status, json.load(resp)\n    except [Subdirectory context discovered: work/acms-project-framework/AGENTS.md]
# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

Resolution returns (policy dict, reason)\n8|where reason ∈ {work_override, agent, project, system}.\n9|\"\"\"\n10|from __future__ import annotations\n11|\n12|import json\n13|from typing import WorkItemRecord\n20|\n21|SCOPES = (\"system\", \"agent\", \"project\", \"work_item\")\n22|INFERENCE_POLICIES = (\"local-only\", \"local-preferred\", \"any\")\n23|REASON_BY_SCOPE = {\n24|    \"work_item\": \"work_override\",\n25|    \"agent\": \"agent\",\n26|    \"project\": \"project\",\n27|    \"system\": \"system\",\n28|}\n29|\n30|\n31|def _policy_dict(rec: ModelPolicyRecord) -> bool(rec.cloud_fallback),\n36|        \"scope\": rec.scope,\n37|        \"policy_id\": rec.policy_id,\n38|    }\n39|\n40|\n41|def _overrides_json(raw: str | None) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n42|    if not raw:\n43|        return None\n44|    try:\n45|        data = json.loads(raw)\n46|    except (TypeError, ValueError):\n47|        return None\n48|    return data if isinstance(data, dict) else None\n49|\n50|\n51|def _apply_overrides(base: dict[str, Any], overrides: dict[str, Any] | None,\n52|                     reason: str) -> dict[str, Any]:\n53|    \"\"\"product.default_model_policy_json / project JSON overrides ride the\n54|    project layer: they are merged into the resolved policy, never silently\n55|    replace unknown keys.\"\"\"\n56|    if not overrides:\n57|        return dict(base, resolution_reason=reason)\n58|    merged = dict(base)\n59|    for key in (\"inference_policy\", \"preferred_model\", \"cloud_fallback\"):\n60|        if overrides.get(key) is not None:\n61|            merged[key] = overrides[key]\n62|    return dict(merged, resolution_reason=reason)\n63|\n64|\n65|async def resolve_policy_for_work_item(db: AsyncSession, work_item_id: str) -> dict[str, ModelPolicyRecord.scope_id == work_item_id,\n75|            )\n76|        )).first()\n77|        if rec is not None:\n78|            return dict(_policy_dict(rec), \"\"\"Local failover chain (plan §19): preferred first, then other healthy\n152|    local models. Cloud is NOT in this chain — the dispatcher appends it only\n153|    when policy allows and all locals are exhausted.\"\"\"\n154|    chain: list[str] = []\n155|    preferred = resolved.get(\"preferred_model\")\n156|    if preferred:\n157|        chain.append(preferred)\n158|    for m in healthy_locals:\n159|        if m not in chain:\n160|            chain.append(m)\n161|    return chain\n162|\n163|def fetch_healthy_local_models() -> list[str]:\n164|    \"\"\"Healthy routable local models via Server Manager /api/v1/model-routes\n165|    (ADR-0015: LLM Manager owns route truth; SM exposes it svc-token-authed).\n166|    Returns logical model names (aliases excluded, cloud engines excluded).\n167|    Fail-open: [] on any error (chain falls back to the preferred model only).\n168|    \"\"\"\n169|    import json\n170|    import urllib.request\n171|\n172|    from .settings import get_settings\n173|\n174|    s = get_settings()\n175|    if not s.server_manager_base_url or not s.server_manager_token:\n176|        return []\n177|    try:\n178|        req = urllib.request.Request(\n179|            s.server_manager_base_url.rstrip(\"/\") + \"/api/v1/model-routes\")\n180|        req.add_header(\"Authorization\", f\"Bearer {s.server_manager_token}\")\n181|        with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=8) as resp:\n182|            data = json.loads(resp.read().decode())\n183|    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — fail-open\n184|        return []\n185|    out: list[str] = []\n186|    for name, rows in (data.get(\"routes\") or {}).items():\n187|        for row in rows or []:\n188|            if not row.get(\"routable\") or row.get(\"is_alias\"):\n189|                continue\n190|            if (row.get(\"health\") or \"\").lower() != \"healthy\":\n191|                continue\n192|            engine = (row.get(\"engine\") or \"\").lower()\n193|            if engine in (\"openrouter\",) or \"openrouter.ai\" in (row.get(\"backend_url\") or \"\"):\n194|                continue  # cloud — never in the local chain\n195|            out.append(name)\n196|            break\n197|    return sorted(set(out))\n198|", "total_lines": 197, "file_size": 7514, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

This\n95|        # makes homepage status/last-contact real without a worker-side pusher.\n96|        await self._poll_agent_heartbeats(s, now_dt)\n97|\n98|    # ------------------------------------------------------- heartbeat poller\n99|\n100|    async def _poll_agent_heartbeats(self, s, now_dt: datetime) -> None:\n101|        \"\"\"Poll-based heartbeat ingestion (plan §16).\"\"\"\n102|        interval = max(15, int(getattr(s, \"heartbeat_interval_seconds\", 60)))\n103|        last = getattr(self, \"_last_heartbeat_poll\", None)\n104|        if last is not None and (now_dt - last).total_seconds() < interval:\n105|            return\n106|        self._last_heartbeat_poll = now_dt\n107|        try:\n108|            from .models import AgentRecord\n109|        except ImportError:\n110|            return\n111|\n112|        async with self._session_factory() as db:\n113|            agents = (await db.scalars(select(AgentRecord))).all()\n114|            # {"status": "success", "output": "250 acms/run_watcher.py\n\"\"\"ADR-0012 runtime-driven completion callback (window-4 plan §5 / Phase C).\n\nProblem this solves (live finding, window 3): the in-instruction COMPLETION\nPROTOCOL depends on the model choosing to comply — the GLM-fast worker\nignored it, and reconciliation became the de-facto completion authority.\nPer plan §C1 the callback must be **infrastructure behavior, not model\nbehavior**: the bridge/runtime layer observes the A2A run's authoritative\nterminal state and performs the callback automatically.\n\nMechanism:\n    At dispatch time ACMS schedules a bounded in-process watcher (one asyncio\n    task per execution task, same durability class as TelemetryScheduler —\n    no Celery/event bus). The watcher polls the bridge run status; on a\n    terminal state (SUCCEEDED/FAILED/CANCELLED) it delivers the EXACT\n    ADR-0012 callback — authenticated HTTP POST to the same\n    /api/v1/callbacks/execution-completion endpoint a worker runtime would\n    use — so the full callback path (token auth, identity binding,\n    idempotency, audit) is exercised, not bypassed.\n\n    The model-facing COMPLETION PROTOCOL hint in the dispatch instruction\n    remains as an additional optional path; duplicate delivery is harmless\n    (the endpoint is idempotent). The reconcile sweep remains the final\n    fallback for lost callbacks, ACMS restarts, and watch expiry.\n\nRetry policy (plan §C3): immediate → 10s → 30s → 60s on transient delivery\nfailure (network/5xx), then stop — reconciliation takes over. Permanent\nrejections (403 identity, 404 unknown task, 503 fail-closed config) stop\nthe watch immediately; retrying cannot fix a contract/config error.\n\nPoll cadence and lifetime are bounded: ACMS_CALLBACK_WATCH_POLL_SECONDS\n(default 30) and ACMS_CALLBACK_WATCH_MAX_SECONDS (default 7200). A\n", "tool_calls_made": 0, "duration_seconds": 0.02}

{"content": "1|\"\"\"RunWatcher tests — ADR-0012 runtime-driven callback (window-4 Phase C).\n2|\n3|Covers plan §C3 (retry policy), §C4 (idempotency semantics), and the dispatch\n4|wiring. All time is injected; no real network, no real sleeps.\n5|\"\"\"\n6|from __future__ import annotations\n7|\n8|import asyncio\n9|\n10|import pytest\n11|\n12|from acms.run_watcher import WatchDeps, testing_registry\n13|\n14|\n15|class FakeBridge:\n16|    def __init__(self, statuses: list[str | Exception]):\n17|        self.statuses = list(statuses)\n18|        self.calls = 0\n19|\n20|    def __call__(self, agent_id: str, run_id: str) -> dict:\n21|        self.calls += 1\n22|        s = self.statuses[min(self.calls - 1, len(self.statuses) - 1)]\n23|        if isinstance(s, Exception):\n24|            raise s\n25|        return s if isinstance(s, dict) else {\"status\": s}\n26|\n27|\n28|class FakePoster:\n29|    \"\"\"Records callback deliveries; scripted results per attempt.\"\"\"\n30|\n31|    def __init__(self, results: list[tuple[bool, bool]] | None = None):\n32|        self.results = list(results or [])\n33|        self.payloads: list[dict] = []\n34|        self.all_ok = results is None\n35|\n36|    def __call__(self, payload: dict) -> tuple[bool, bool]:\n37|        self.payloads.append(payload)\n38|        if self.all_ok:\n39|            return True, False\n40|        return self.results.pop(0) if self.results else (True, False)\n41|\n42|\n43|\n44|async def _mk_task(db, *, status=\"RUNNING\", run_id=\"run_x\") -> tuple[str, str]:\n45|    \"\"\"One WorkItem + ExecutionTask row (work_item_id is NOT NULL). {"status": "success", "output": "def _idempotency_key(work_item_id: str, assignment_id: str | None,\n                     client_key: str | None) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Stable dedupe key. Client key wins; else the open assignment; else work.\"\"\"\n    basis = client_key or assignment_id or work_item_id\n    return hashlib.sha256(f\"acms-dispatch:{basis}\".encode()).hexdigest()[:32]\n\n\nasync def _check_worker_busy(db: AsyncSession, agent_id: str, work_item_id: str) -> dict | None:\n    \"\"\"One task per worker (plan §3; ADR-0013): refuse a NEW dispatch when the\n    target agent already has a non-terminal execution task for a DIFFERENT\n    work item. Returns the blocking detail or None.\"\"\"\n    rows = (await db.scalars(\n        select(ExecutionTaskRecord)\n        .where(ExecutionTaskRecord.agent_id == agent_id,\n               ExecutionTaskRecord.status == \"RUNNING\")\n        .order_by(ExecutionTaskRecord.started_at.desc())\n        .limit(10)\n    )).all()\n    for t in rows:\nasync def _find_existing_task(db: AsyncSession, dedupe_key: str) -> Any | None:\n    \"\"\"The most recent task created from this dedupe key.\n\n    The dedupe ledger lives in the durable EXECUTION_DISPATCHED event metadata\n    (``dedupe_key`` → ``task_id``) — external_task_id now carries the A2A run\n    id (ADR-0012 callback binding), so it can no longer double as the idem-\n    potency ledger. The event log is the same durability class ACMS already\n    relies on for reassignment counting (PR #32 pattern).\n    \"\"\"\n    rows = (await db.scalars(\n        select(AgentEventRecord)\n        .where(AgentEventRecord.event_type == \"EXECUTION_DISPATCHED\")\n        .order_by(AgentEventRecord.timestamp.desc())\n        .limit(200)\n    )).all()\n    for ev in rows:  # newest first\n        try:\n            meta = json.loads(ev.metadata_json) if ev.metadata_json else {}\n        except (TypeError, ValueError):\n            continue\n        if meta.get(\"dedupe_key\") == dedupe_key and meta.get(\"task_id\"):\n            return await db.get(ExecutionTaskRecord, meta[\"task_id\"])\n    return None\n\n\nasync def dispatch_work(db: AsyncSession, *, work_item_id: str,\n\n", "tool_calls_made": 0, "duration_seconds": 0.02}

{"status": "success", "output": "        raise DispatchError(\"agent-mismatch\", 409,\n                            {\"active_assignment_agent\": active.agent_id})\n\n    # ---- 2. idempotency: a retry must never double-dispatch ----------------\n    dedupe_key = _idempotency_key(work_item_id, active.assignment_id if active else None,\n                                  idempotency_key)\n    existing = await _find_existing_task(db, dedupe_key)\n    if existing is not None and existing.status == \"RUNNING\":\n        return {\"dispatched\": False, \"reason\": \"duplicate\",\n                \"task_id\": existing.task_id, \"external_task_id\": None,\n                \"budget_check\": None}\n\n    # ---- 2a. one-task-per-worker (plan §3; ADR-0013) ------------------------\n    busy = await _check_worker_busy(db, str(target_agent), work_item_id)\n    if busy is not None:\n        await add_event(\n            db,\n            event_type=\"EXECUTION_REJECTED\",\n            actor_source=\"dispatch\",\n            agent_id=target_agent,\n            work_key=work.work_key,\n            assignment_key=active.assignment_key if active else None,\n            summary=(f\"409 WORKER_BUSY: {str(target_agent)[:8]} already executes \"\n                     f\"{busy['blocking_work_item_id'][:8]} — scheduler must pick \"\n                     f\"another idle worker\")[:512],\n            metadata={\"worker_busy\": busy, \"work_item_id\": work_item_id,\n                      \"dedupe_key\": dedupe_key},\n        )\n        await db.commit()\n        return {\"dispatched\": False, \"reason\": \"worker_busy\", \"worker_busy\": busy,\n                \"task_id\": None, \"external_task_id\": None, \"budget_check\": None}\n\n", "tool_calls_made": 0, "duration_seconds": 0.02}

{"status": "success", "output": "def schedule_run_watch(task_id: str, *, enabled: bool | None = None) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Schedule the runtime-driven callback watcher for one execution task.\n\n    Called by dispatch_service after a successful bridge dispatch. Never\n    raises — watch scheduling must not break dispatch (failures log only).\n    Returns True when a watcher was scheduled, False when disabled/duplicate.\n    \"\"\"\n    from .settings import get_settings\n\n    try:\n        s = get_settings()\n        if enabled is None:\n            enabled = s.callback_watch_enabled and bool(s.callback_token) and bool(s.callback_base_url)\n        if not enabled:\n            return False\n        return _registry.schedule(task_id, _production_deps())\n    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001\n        logger.exception(\"scheduling run watcher failed for task %s\", task_id[:12])\n        return False\n\n\ndef testing_registry() -> RunWatchRegistry:\n    \"\"\"Fresh registry for tests (never touches the module singleton).\"\"\"\n    return RunWatchRegistry()\n\n", "tool_calls_made": 0, "duration_seconds": 0.02}

2. agent preference\n+    if agent_id is not None:\n+        rec = (await db.scalars(\n+            select(ModelPolicyRecord).where(\n+                ModelPolicyRecord.scope == \"agent\",\n+                ModelPolicyRecord.scope_id == agent_id,\n+            )\n+        )).first()\n+        if rec is not None:\n+            return dict(_policy_dict(rec), resolution_reason=\"agent\")\n+\n+    #
